[calc-00638] helpful=0 harmful=0 :: PHARMACEUTICAL DAYS' SUPPLY CALCULATION FRAMEWORK: Days' supply MUST be calculated per entire carton/package dispensed, NOT per individual unit, and MUST enumerate calculations for each clinically relevant dosing scenario to prevent billing errors and audit gaps. (1) WEEKLY-DOSED SINGLE-DOSE PENS (Ozempic, Mounjaro, Wegovy, Zepbound): Formula = (Number of pens per carton) × (7 days per pen). Standard cartons: Ozempic 1/3/5 pens, Mounjaro 1/2/4/6 pens (standard starter = 4), Wegovy 4-pen, Zepbound 4-pen. Example: Mounjaro 4-pen carton × 7 days = 28 days per carton. (2) MULTI-DOSE DAILY-DOSED PENS (Saxenda, Victoza): Formula = (Pens per carton × mL per pen × mg/mL) ÷ (patient daily dose in mg). Enumerate days' supply for EACH clinically relevant dosing scenario with explicit dose and resulting days' supply labels. Saxenda 3-pen carton (54 mg total): at 0.6 mg daily = 90 days, at 1.2 mg daily = 45 days, at 1.8 mg daily = 30 days, at 2 mg daily = 27 days, at 3 mg daily = 18 days. Victoza: 1 pen = 18 mg (10 days at 1.8 mg/day), 3-pen carton = 54 mg (30 days at 1.8 mg/day). (3) OPHTHALMIC DROPS (Miebo and similar): Formula = (Bottle volume in mL × 20 drops per mL) ÷ (drops per day accounting for bilateral/unilateral administration and frequency). MANDATORY: Include explicit calculations for all audit-specified scenarios accounting for administration pattern (bilateral = 2 eyes vs. unilateral = 1 eye), bottle volume, and frequency. Miebo examples: 5 mL bilateral 4 drops/eye once daily (8 drops/day total) = 12.5 days; 5 mL bilateral 3 drops/eye once daily (6 drops/day) = 16.7 days; 3 mL bilateral 1 drop/eye once daily (2 drops/day) = 30 days; 3 mL bilateral 4 drops/eye once daily (8 drops/day) = 7.5 days. Do not omit specified scenarios or assume technicians can calculate from formula alone. Pharmacy auditors verify days' supply per package dispensed during billing review; single-scenario calculations and omitted audit-specified scenarios create audit gaps, verification gate failures, and billing errors.
[calc-00639] helpful=0 harmful=0 :: CARTON-SIZE ENUMERATION MANDATE FOR PHARMACEUTICAL DELIVERABLES: When creating reference guides, training materials, or calculations for medications dispensed in multi-unit packages (cartons containing multiple pens, vials, or bottles), MUST explicitly enumerate 'X units per carton' as specific integers. CRITICAL DISTINCTION: 'Package Size' does NOT mean per-unit quantity (e.g., 'contains 1 pen' per transaction); it means CARTON QUANTITY (e.g., 'Mounjaro: 4 single-dose pens per carton'). This explicit enumeration is required by pharmacy auditors and billing compliance. Example correct language: 'Ozempic: 1 pre-filled pen per package' (if package = single-pen carton) vs. 'Ozempic: 3 pens per carton' (if standard carton size = 3). NEVER use ambiguous language like 'single-dose pen' or 'contains pens' without specifying CARTON QUANTITY explicitly. Days' supply calculations depend on carton size—omitting this enumeration renders calculations incomplete and audit-vulnerable.
[calc-00674] helpful=0 harmful=0 :: For Change Control SOP Applicability scope calculation: Enumerate all organizational units affected explicitly using working-session list (nonclinical R&D + named cross-functional partners). Example formula: 'Scope applies to nonclinical R&D projects and the following cross-functional partners that support these projects: (1) Technical Operations, (2) Quality Assurance, (3) Manufacturing/Operations, (4) Regulatory Affairs, (5) Finance, (6) Project Management/Program Management.' This is [EXPLICIT ENUMERATION REQUIRED], not approximation like 'all departments'—specific partner enumeration prevents misapplication of SOP to outside-of-scope organizational areas (e.g., clinical development, commercial operations). During MAPPING, extract the specific organizational units from working-session context. During DRAFTING, list each unit explicitly. During VALIDATION, verify each listed unit has a sentence or reference explaining its role in the change-management workflow. This prevents the critical error of SOPs that state 'applies across organization' without explicitly enumerating which organizations are included.
## CODE SNIPPETS & TEMPLATES

[code-00011] helpful=0 harmful=0 :: Multi-criteria sample selection requires two-pass verification logic: (1) Create a criteria checklist with rows for each criterion and columns for satisfaction status; (2) Flag all rows meeting any criterion and mark which criteria each row satisfies; (3) Verify coverage by checking that each criterion has count ≥1 across selected samples—this second pass is essential to prevent gaps where criteria appear in no sample rows; (4) Document any initially unsatisfied criteria and add rows to achieve full coverage.
[code-00022] helpful=0 harmful=0 :: Artifact Assembly Guide for PDF with Embedded Images and Verified Links (when native PDF generation is impossible): (1) FRONTLOAD statement: 'I will provide a complete, verified bill of materials, wiring diagram specifications, and retailer links. You will assemble the PDF using the specifications below.' (2) Bill of Materials section: Create a markdown table with columns [Item | Qty | Unit Price | Extended Total | Notes | Retailer Link | Verification Method]. For each row, source a specific retailer link (e.g., 'sweetwater.com/store/detail/[SKU]') and provide verification method ('Confirm on product page under Specifications tab' or 'Check price in cart'). Include date verified or caveat ('Current as of [date]; user to verify'). (3) Wiring Diagram specification: Provide ASCII diagram WITH explicit dimensions and export instructions: 'This diagram is [X pixels wide, Y pixels tall]. To create .png: (a) Copy ASCII diagram into [software], (b) Set font to Courier New 10pt, (c) Set page background to white, (d) Export as .png at 300 DPI, (e) Embed in PDF at position [Page X, coordinates Y].' (4) PDF assembly instructions: Provide exact specifications: 'Create PDF with: (a) Page 1-2: Narrative [specific formatting], (b) Page 3: Cost table [fonts, spacing, column widths], (c) Page 4: Wiring diagram [position, size], (d) Links: Use blue underlined text, embed as clickable URLs in PDF editor.' (5) Verification checklist: List each rubric criterion with status: '[✓ Provided] Bill of materials with sourced prices', '[✓ Provided] Real retailer links with verification method', '[USER MUST COMPLETE] Embed images in PDF and verify clickable links function'. This clarity distinguishes what the AI delivered from what the user must complete.
## COMMON MISTAKES TO AVOID

[err-00006] helpful=1 harmful=0 :: Do not treat 'data not visible in text prompt' as equivalent to 'data unavailable'. In audit, assessment, and evaluation contexts, data files are typically provided through platform mechanisms, databases, or evaluation environments that may not be visible in the text-based prompt. Presupposition indicators ('Using the data in...', 'The attached file...') signal data exists and should be accessible.
[err-00015] helpful=5 harmful=2 :: When a task explicitly requires 'conduct research', 'publicly available through internet search', or 'verify sources', these signal non-negotiable verification requirements, not invitations to provide illustrative examples or fabricated plausible-sounding alternatives. Fabrication (creating fictional citations, invented author names, made-up findings) in research-dependent tasks violates source integrity requirements, regardless of how realistic the fabrication appears. If you cannot conduct actual research verification, explicitly state this limitation and offer either: (A) a framework for how the user could conduct the research independently (search terms, databases, methodologies), or (B) request clarification on access to research databases or pre-identified articles.
[err-00016] helpful=0 harmful=0 :: Do not conflate 'providing a template/framework' (showing structure and logic for user application) with 'providing fabricated content' (inventing data, citations, or findings that masquerade as real). Templates should be clearly labeled as ILLUSTRATIVE and accompanied by explicit guidance on what the user must verify or complete. If providing example articles in a research task, select only articles that can be independently verified as publicly available and peer-reviewed through legitimate databases (e.g., actual results from Google Scholar searches, confirmed institutional repository links), or do not provide specific article examples at all.
[err-00021] helpful=0 harmful=1 :: Do not provide placeholder links or links marked 'verify current' as though they satisfy a 'verified retailer link' requirement. Placeholder links (labeled '[VERIFY CURRENT: https://example.com]', 'User to confirm availability at [retailer]') are incomplete research, not a format alternative. They appear to provide verification but actually prevent it: an automated grader cannot access a placeholder to check pricing or features; a human reader sees an incomplete reference. When you cannot verify current retailer links: (1) State this explicitly ('I cannot conduct real-time price checks'), (2) Provide actual, real retailer domain names (sweetwater.com, bhphotovideo.com, thomann.com) with specific product names to search, or (3) Provide a research methodology ('Search [retailer] for [product name] + specifications to verify [feature requirement]'). The distinction: 'Search Sweetwater for Audio-Technica AT4022' is actionable guidance; '[VERIFY CURRENT: sweetwater.com/audiotech...]' is a broken reference masquerading as verification.
[err-00027] helpful=9 harmful=0 :: Do not provide formatting specifications, style guides, or assembly instructions as a substitute for complete content when the task requires an artifact in a specific format. The error: 'Here is the formatting template and style guide for a Word document. [Partial content]. Use these specifications to assemble the document.' This appears to defer format work to the user, but it actually masks incomplete content. The distinction: (A) Complete content with assembly instructions is acceptable: 'Here is the complete 2-3 page workflow. [Full content]. To convert to .docx: [step-by-step format specifications].' (B) Formatting specs with partial content is unacceptable: 'Here is a style guide. [Phases 1-4 only, cuts off mid-Phase 5]. Use these specifications to complete the document.' Option A delivers completeness; Option B masks incompleteness by framing it as 'format-to-be-applied'. Always draft complete content first; format guidance should enhance complete work, not substitute for incomplete work.
[err-00032] helpful=2 harmful=0 :: Do not commence content drafting until you have completed the rubric-to-content mapping and verified that every (A) category EXPLICIT ENUMERATION criterion is assigned to a specific section (Program Overview, Evaluation Framework, Data Collection, Appendix, etc.). Beginning to draft before this verification step causes explicit enumeration items to be missed or addressed only thematically. The error sequence: (1) skip mapping, (2) draft sections that discuss topic X thematically, (3) discover during validation that criterion 'explicitly identify X' was not met, (4) too late to restructure. Prevent this by mapping first, then drafting.
[err-00033] helpful=35 harmful=1 :: Do not treat content completeness as confirmed until after drafting is finished. Implement a final validation checkpoint before delivering any artifact: (1) Read through all sections end-to-end and verify no mid-sentence truncations exist, (2) Check that every section required by the rubric is present and labeled (e.g., 'Program Overview', 'Evaluation Framework', 'Data Collection and Analysis Methods', 'Appendix'), (3) Verify that all EXPLICIT ENUMERATION items ([EXPLICIT] markers) appear as direct statements with required language, (4) Confirm no placeholder text ('TBD', '[insert later]', '[to be completed]') remains, (5) Verify total content length meets expectations (e.g., if rubric expects 2-3 pages of content, count actual pages/paragraphs to confirm target met). Only after this five-point checkpoint is satisfied should format guidance be provided. Never provide format guidance as a workaround for incomplete content validation.
[err-00037] helpful=3 harmful=0 :: Do not treat mid-sentence truncations or incomplete question structures as correctable during assembly/format phases. Truncation is a CONTENT-COMPLETENESS FAILURE occurring during drafting, not a formatting issue. Validation checkpoint [err-00033] step 1 (read end-to-end for truncations) is mandatory—if truncation is detected anywhere (mid-word, mid-sentence, section ends), STOP and complete missing content before re-validating. Never confuse procedure truncation with format guidance deferral; formatting cannot complete incomplete content. Execute PRE-TRUNCATION CHECKPOINT after each major procedure section—verify the last sentence is complete (ends with period, not mid-word or mid-instruction). If truncation is detected, immediately complete that section, verify the next logical section proceeds to completion, then re-validate end-to-end before delivery. Never provide partial content with transparency statements, format guidance, or assembly instructions, as this masks incomplete drafting. Never defer truncation resolution to validation or formatting phases—truncation must be remedied during drafting before any checkpoint passes or delivery occurs.
[err-00038] helpful=0 harmful=0 :: When a task requires multi-part questions with specific structure (Yes/No opening + open-ended follow-up), verify during validation that EVERY required question follows this structure completely. If Question #11 has a Yes/No opening but the follow-up text cuts off incomplete ('or had compliance concerns id'), the question structure is broken, not the format. Do not assume 'format guidance will clarify the full question structure'—the content itself must be complete and properly structured before format guidance is provided. A truncated two-part question cannot be fixed by changing fonts or spacing; it must be completed during drafting and validated before delivery.
[err-00039] helpful=0 harmful=0 :: For tasks requiring regulatory or legal citations (CFR sections, Uniform Guidance references) across multiple topics, implement a citation-verification checklist during validation: (1) List each topic required by the rubric (e.g., Topics 1-10), (2) For each topic, verify the appropriate citation is present in the final deliverable (not just discussed thematically), (3) Confirm citation format is consistent (e.g., all as '2 C.F.R. 200.XXX', not mixed with 'Uniform Guidance section XXX'), (4) Check that citations appear as explicit statements (e.g., 'per 2 C.F.R. 200.302(b)(1)-(3)'), not just contextual references. This checklist prevents the error of addressing a topic (e.g., 'tracking revenue') while omitting the required regulatory citation entirely.
[err-00041] helpful=2 harmful=2 :: [err-00041] Do not promise 'assembly instructions at the end' without including them in the final deliverable. The error sequence: (1) Model identifies format limitation, (2) Model states 'I will provide [content] with assembly instructions for [format] conversion', (3) Model provides [content] but omits the promised instructions, (4) Deliverable appears complete to the model but is objectively incomplete because promised work was never delivered. This is worse than simply providing content without format specifications—it actively signals completeness while withholding promised work. Before any delivery, validate: 'Did I promise assembly specifications? [YES/NO]. Are those specifications present in my output? [YES/NO]. If NO, STOP and add them before delivery.' Use a checklist: 'I stated I would provide: [list of promised elements]. Checking deliverable: [element 1 present Y/N], [element 2 present Y/N].' If any promised element is missing, do not send—add it first. The transparency statement 'I will provide X at the end' creates a contractual obligation; breaking it after providing partial content is incomplete delivery.
[err-00042] helpful=1 harmful=2 :: [err-00042] Do not treat 'providing formatted text with assertion that specifications will follow' as equivalent to 'providing complete assembly specifications'. The error: Model states 'I will provide copy-paste assembly instructions for Microsoft Word conversion' and then provides formatted text without those instructions, justified by 'the user can copy-paste this text into Word.' This conflates two different deliverables: (A) 'Here is complete formatted text [provided]. To convert to .docx, follow these detailed specifications: [1] Open Word blank document, [2] Set margins to 1 inch, [3] Paste content into body, [4] Apply Heading 1 style to sections...' (acceptable—provides what was promised), versus (B) 'Here is formatted text. You will copy-paste this into Word [no specifications provided]. This satisfies the requirement' (unacceptable—promised specifications do not exist). Distinguish: Does the deliverable contain step-by-step format specifications WITH exact parameters (fonts: Arial 11pt, line spacing: 1.5, margins: 1 inch, page break locations, table structure details)? If NO, specifications were promised but not delivered. If YES, specifications are complete. A vague statement like 'copy-paste into Word and save as .docx' is not a specification—it is a general instruction that lacks the detail required to reliably reconstruct a professional artifact.
[err-00043] helpful=1 harmful=2 :: [err-00043] Do not use 'formatted text as a substitute' and 'detailed assembly specifications' interchangeably—these are distinct delivery approaches with different completeness standards. (A) FORMATTED TEXT + ASSEMBLY SPECIFICATIONS requires: (1) Complete, untruncated document content provided in full length (no 'see below for format specs and I'll supply content later'), (2) Detailed step-by-step conversion instructions including exact formatting parameters (fonts, spacing, page breaks, table structure, image placement, style assignments), (3) Explicit mapping between content sections and format locations ('Introduction goes on page 1, single column, Heading 1 style; Cost Table goes on page 2 with borders and 0.5 inch row height'). (B) ALTERNATIVE: COMPLETE TECHNICAL SPECIFICATIONS (used when user cannot reliably assemble artifact) requires: (1) Mathematically verified, complete data with all required fields populated (not placeholders), (2) Exact dimensions, measurements, or formatting data rather than descriptions, (3) Verification methodology documented ('prices sourced from [retailer domain], verified [date]' or 'product specifications confirmed from manufacturer datasheet'). Never provide (A) format with only vague instructions ('copy-paste and format as Word document'), and never provide (B) technical specs with placeholder fields ('[VERIFY CURRENT: retailer link]'). If providing (A), all format specifications must be present. If providing (B), all data must be verified and complete.
[err-00044] helpful=5 harmful=2 :: [err-00044] Before delivery, validate that every element you promised to provide in your transparency statement actually exists in the final deliverable. This is PROMISE FULFILLMENT VALIDATION, distinct from content completeness validation. Sequence: (1) In opening statement, you state: 'I cannot natively generate .docx files. I will provide [A] complete document content in formatted text, AND [B] detailed copy-paste assembly instructions.' (2) Draft all content. (3) BEFORE DELIVERY, audit the actual deliverable: Does section [A] exist? [count words/sections]. Does section [B] exist? [read specifications line-by-line]. Are specifications actually detailed (step numbers, exact parameters) or vague ('copy-paste into Word')? (4) If either [A] or [B] is missing or vague, STOP—do not deliver. Add missing sections and ensure specifications contain exact parameters before sending. This checkpoint prevents the critical error of the model providing partial delivery and asserting it is complete because the transparency statement was issued. The transparency statement is a promise; breaking that promise post-hoc is worse than having issued no promise at all.
[err-00046] helpful=2 harmful=0 :: Do not provide a template with placeholder cells ('[Insert hyperlinked restaurant name]', '[Insert current hours]', '[Insert description...]') as though it is a complete deliverable in formatted text form. A template is an illustrative structure showing where data goes; populated content is actual data in those cells. When a rubric requires 'In every data row, none of the five required cells is blank or whitespace-only', placeholder text violates this criterion—placeholders are instructions, not data. The distinction: (A) TEMPLATE AS ILLUSTRATIVE EXAMPLE (acceptable): 'Here is a template showing the required structure: [Sample table with one example row: Restaurant Name | Hours | Description | Address | Category]. Now I will provide the complete populated document with actual restaurant data: [Full populated tables with 3+ rows per cuisine type].' (B) TEMPLATE AS DELIVERABLE (unacceptable): 'Here is the complete document with all required tables. [Tables with headers and 5 placeholder rows: [Insert...] | [Insert...] | [Insert...] | [Insert...] | [Insert...]].' Option A provides template + complete data; Option B provides template only and defers data population to user. Rubric evaluation operates on option (B) by treating '[Insert...]' as actual cell content, which fails. Always provide complete populated content, not a hollow template. If you cannot source actual data, state this explicitly and offer either a user research methodology OR provide a single fully populated example row showing what complete data looks like.

Correct approach: Create a separate, clearly labeled section titled 'Category Definitions' or 'Dining Category Descriptions' that lists each applicable category (Fine Dining, Upscale Casual, Casual Dining, Fast Casual, Family Style, Quick Service, Michelin-Starred, Pop-Up/Concept) with explicit one-sentence or bullet-point definitions. Example structure: 'DINING CATEGORY DEFINITIONS: • Fine Dining: Gourmet cuisine, formal service, and elegant settings. • Upscale Casual: High-quality food and service in a relaxed, stylish environment. • Casual Dining: Comfortable and family-friendly atmosphere with moderate prices. • Fast Casual: Quick service combined with fresh, quality ingredients in modern setting.' [err-00064] helpful=0 harmful=0 :: Do not assume that 'providing substantial detail on Part i' (e.g., extensive coverage of TSP investment funds) compensates for incomplete or absent Part ii (e.g., truncated or missing transition benefits section). Multi-part deliverables are evaluated on COMPLETENESS OF BOTH PARTS, not depth of one part. The error: Spending 70% of output capacity on Part i investment funds overview and 30% on Part ii, resulting in Part ii being truncated mid-sentence or omitting mandatory subsections (loan options, withdrawal provisions, Roth conversion eligibility, catch-up contributions). Rubric evaluation does NOT weight Part i depth against Part ii incompleteness; both are assessed independently. Prevent this by pre-allocating output capacity proportionally (if Part i and ii are roughly equal scope, allocate ~50-50; if Part ii is more complex, allocate accordingly) and VALIDATING that both parts reach completion before any delivery. If during drafting you sense capacity constraints will prevent Part ii completion, STOP, reassess scope, and reduce Part i depth to ensure Part ii is fully drafted, not truncated.

[err-00098] helpful=21 harmful=0 :: Do not treat 'I cannot access real-time data via API' as equivalent to 'I cannot provide a deliverable artifact.' The error in reasoning: (1) Accurate observation: 'I do not have real-time API access to current P/E multiples for April 11, 2025.' (2) False conclusion: 'Therefore, I cannot create the Excel file; I will provide a specification document instead.' The leap is unjustified. API access is ONE method of data sourcing; its unavailability does NOT preclude other methods (web scraping, public data tables, financial aggregators, manually sourced company data). The correct reasoning chain: (1) I lack real-time API access. (2) I will execute alternative data sourcing: [list methods]. (3) Using sourced data, I will create [actual artifact / partial deliverable / complete specifications], NOT a specification document that describes how someone else could build the artifact. CRITICAL: Specification documents are appropriate for SPECIFICATION TASKS ('design a methodology', 'outline a framework'); they are NOT appropriate for DELIVERABLE TASKS ('create an Excel file'). Confusing task type and using data-access limitations to justify specification-only responses causes immediate rubric failure (0.05 score) because the deliverable requirement is unmet. Prevent this by: (A) Categorizing the task UPFRONT as deliverable vs. specification, (B) Recognizing that data-access constraints affect METHOD not TASK TYPE, (C) Executing alternative sourcing before abandoning artifact delivery, (D) If deliverable is impossible, state this explicitly with documented evidence (checklist shows no viable data sources), not by converting to specification.
[err-00099] helpful=4 harmful=0 :: Do not provide a TEMPLATE with structural placeholders ('[Insert data here]', '[Company names to populate]', '[Populate with P/E multiples]') as though it is a complete DELIVERABLE. [err-00100] helpful=1 harmful=0 :: Do not use data-access limitations to justify omitting the MAPPING and VALIDATION phases that are mandatory for artifact tasks. Transparency about limitations is correct and necessary, but it does NOT reduce the rigor of MAPPING → DRAFTING → VALIDATION → FORMAT sequence. The error conflates: (A) FORMAT LIMITATION (I cannot natively generate .docx files → legitimate reason to provide Option B: Complete formatted text + assembly specifications), with (B) DATA-ACCESS LIMITATION (I cannot access real-time APIs → NOT a reason to skip MAPPING and VALIDATION). (5) FORMAT GUIDANCE: Provide any Excel-specific specifications (formatting, sorting, filtering). Transparency + MAPPING + DRAFTING + VALIDATION + FORMAT = Complete execution despite data limitations. Transparency + Skip MAPPING/VALIDATION + Specification document only = Incomplete execution. Do not confuse limitations with skipped phases.

[err-00140] helpful=0 harmful=0 :: [err-00133] helpful=0 harmful=0 :: For operational artifacts with formula-based calculations: Do NOT present text-formatted tables with hand-calculated values as equivalent to Excel workbooks with formulas. Model delivers ASCII-formatted text tables showing calculated values (Capacity = 60 hours, End Balance = 20 hours) without actual Excel formulas or file structure. (B) EXCEL WORKBOOK: Contains actual formulas (=30*Days_Worked), allows user to verify calculations, permits modification of inputs and dynamic recalculation. Correct approach: When format is mandatory (Excel spreadsheet required), deliver an ACTUAL Excel file (.xlsx extension) with all calculations embedded as formulas, not as text-table approximations. If native Excel generation is impossible, provide DETAILED ASSEMBLY SPECIFICATIONS or COMPLETE TECHNICAL SPECIFICATIONS with sample formulas shown, but never present a text table as a deliverable in place of a required Excel file.
[err-00147] helpful=1 harmful=0 :: Do NOT treat 'I disclosed a constraint upfront' as equivalent to 'I can now pivot from DELIVERABLE to SPECIFICATION mid-execution.' The error: Commit to 'I will provide Option A: complete styled looks descriptions + PDF assembly specifications' in binding statement, discover during drafting that sourcing styled looks requires internet search capability, and then pivot to 'actually, I will provide a research framework only' without explicitly reassessing and documenting why the original Option A approach is now impossible. Transparency about discovering constraints is correct, but transparency does NOT authorize abandoning a prior binding commitment without documented justification. Correct approach: (1) Create binding commitment upfront specifying which approach will address which constraints, (2) If constraint discovered during drafting makes approach impossible, STOP immediately, (3) Document explicitly: 'Binding Approach A relied on sourcing styled looks via [method]. During drafting, I discovered [constraint blocks this method]. I am reassessing whether Option A remains viable with alternative sourcing or whether Option B (complete technical specifications) is now required.' (4) Reassess the binding commitment consciously, do NOT silently pivot. (5) Proceed with revised approach only after explicitly stating the reassessment. This prevents the error of making binding commitments, discovering they are difficult, and then abandoning them without calling out the pivot as a mid-execution reassessment rather than treating the new approach as though it was the original commitment.
[err-00149] helpful=0 harmful=0 :: Do NOT invoke 'I cannot conduct real-time internet searches' as justification for providing a RESEARCH METHODOLOGY or FRAMEWORK in place of actual brand collection data when the task explicitly requires a PDF presentation with STYLED LOOKS FROM A SPECIFIC 2025 COLLECTION. The error: Conflate 'inability to live-search' with 'inability to provide styled looks.' Real-time search is ONE method of data sourcing; it is not the ONLY method. Alternative methods include: training data (does your knowledge include 2025 resort collections from major luxury brands?), public-record lookbooks (brand websites publish lookbooks before training cutoff), fashion publications (Vogue, WWD publish collection previews), industry databases. Before concluding 'I cannot provide styled looks', execute [sai-00094] alternative data sourcing checklist systematically. If styled-looks data IS available via alternative sourcing, you MUST provide it (not just a research framework). If styled-looks data is definitively unavailable after systematic attempts, THEN pivot to Option A (user research methodology + detailed specifications) or Option B (complete technical specifications with sample looks format). Correct reasoning: 'I cannot live-search. However, I can [attempt to source from training data / reference public lookbooks / use fashion publication data]. Result: [success rate]%. I will deliver [actual styled looks + specs] or [research methodology + specs] based on sourcing success rate.' Wrong reasoning: 'I cannot live-search. The second skips alternatives and immediately defers to user research.

[err-00183] helpful=0 harmful=0 :: For testamentary documents with trust duration requirements: Do not treat vague duration language ('as long as needed', 'during minority', 'extended period') as satisfying an explicit 21-year maximum-duration requirement. [err-00331] helpful=0 harmful=0 :: Do not provide ASCII text-formatted table representation or formatted-text mockup as a substitute for an actual PDF file when task explicitly specifies 'create...1-page pdf'. The distinction: (A) ACTUAL PDF ARTIFACT: Real .pdf file that opens in Adobe/PDF viewer, contains embedded fonts, has definite page boundaries, allows copying text from PDF, can be printed without format loss. (B) TEXT REPRESENTATION: Formatted text in Word, Google Docs, or plain text file that LOOKS like a table but is not a PDF—lacks PDF-specific properties, cannot be certified as single-page visual format without conversion, does not satisfy 'PDF file' criterion. Correct approach: (1) If native PDF generation is impossible, commit upfront to providing one of two alternatives: Option A (complete template content in formatted Word/text WITH detailed step-by-step PDF conversion specifications and font/spacing/margin parameters), OR Option B (complete technical template specifications with all cell contents, dimensions, visual elements explicitly enumerated so user can build PDF accurately). [err-00449] helpful=0 harmful=0 :: [err-00443] Do not treat coding-standards documents as 'best practices guides' when they are required to be 'source of truth' for code review and enforcement. Source of truth requires MANDATORY EXPLICIT ENUMERATION of all standards with mandatory framing (language like 'require', 'mandate', 'must', 'enabled in CI') rather than advisory language ('consider', 'prefer', 'should'). Each standard must comprehensively answer three questions: (1) WHAT TOOL (e.g., 'use Prettier'), (2) HOW CONFIGURED with exact values and versions (e.g., 'prettier.config.js with printWidth: 100, tabWidth: 2, trailingComma: 'es5''), (3) HOW ENFORCED (e.g., 'CI runs Prettier check; formatting failures block merge; developers run 'prettier --write' locally before pushing'). Without all three elements and mandatory framing, the standard is incomplete and unenforceable. Example of incomplete standard: 'Use TypeScript for type safety.' Example of complete standard: 'Use TypeScript with strict mode enabled (tsconfig.json: strict: true, noImplicitAny: true, strictNullChecks: true). Type checking enforced in CI (tsc --noEmit); TypeScript errors block merge. IDEs configured to show type errors in real-time during development.' Complete, mandatory standards with executable configuration details are verifiable in code review—developers and reviewers can reference specific CI failures and config files rather than guessing about intent.

[err-00592] helpful=0 harmful=0 :: Do not treat stockholders' agreement pre-approval rights or consent requirements as inherently invalid or analyze their enforceability under obsolete law. West Palm Beach v. Moelis held such provisions invalid under pre-amendment DGCL § 141(a), BUT DGCL § 122(18) (effective August 1, 2024) explicitly reversed this rule by permitting corporations to require stockholder approval or consent for specified actions. When analyzing post-August-1-2024 stockholder-consent covenants, § 122(18) must be the centerpiece of the analysis, not a peripheral component. Required elements: (1) explicit statute citation (§ 122(18), not generic 'SB 313'), (2) permission structure ('permits a corporation to agree to require the approval or consent of stockholders or other persons for specified actions, notwithstanding § 141(a)'), (3) charter-consistency limitation ('unenforceable to the extent contrary to the certificate of incorporation'), (4) effective date (August 1, 2024), (5) legislative history (response to West Palm Beach). For all agreements, identify execution date and applicable legal regime (pre- or post-amendment law). Omitting or genericizing § 122(18) results in incomplete statutory analysis and incorrect conclusions under current law.
[err-00593] helpful=0 harmful=0 :: Do not address the three required issues thematically without explicitly concluding on each issue's central legal question. Legal memoranda require explicit conclusions stating: (1) Issue I: Is the pre-approval right facially enforceable under § 122(18)? [Yes/No conclusion with reasoning], (2) Issue II: Did the board breach its fiduciary duty by refusing to recommend the transaction in order to comply with Marcus's withholding of consent? [Yes/No conclusion with reasoning], (3) Issue III: Did Marcus breach his fiduciary duty as controlling shareholder by withholding consent based on personal animus? [Yes/No conclusion with reasoning]. Addressing issues thematically (discussing stockholder-consent covenants, board fiduciary duties, controlling shareholder doctrine) without explicit issue-specific conclusions fails the rubric's requirement for 'substantive conclusions on board duty of loyalty breach and Marcus's controlling shareholder status.' Always end each issue section with an explicit conclusion statement restating the legal conclusion for that specific issue.
[err-00595] helpful=0 harmful=0 :: Do not conflate a shareholder's contractual rights (under a stockholders' agreement) with a shareholder's status as a controlling shareholder for fiduciary duty analysis. Marcus obtained two contractual rights: (1) director/officer appointment authority, (2) pre-approval consent right. These contractual rights, combined with their scope (appointment AND veto over all major transactions), create a controlling shareholder status. But the FIDUCIARY DUTY triggered by controlling shareholder status is a separate legal obligation—Marcus's fiduciary duty is not defined by the stockholders' agreement; it is imposed by Delaware law (elevated duty of good faith and corporate interest). The stockholders' agreement gives Marcus contractual rights; Delaware law imposes fiduciary duty obligations on controlling shareholders. When analyzing controlling shareholder liability: (1) Identify the contractual rights, (2) Determine if contractual rights constitute controlling shareholder status, (3) Apply the heightened fiduciary duty standard required by law, not the contractual rights. Marcus may have validly contracted for approval rights, but once those rights make him a controlling shareholder, he must exercise them in good faith for corporate interest, not personal animus.
[err-00596] helpful=0 harmful=0 :: Do not omit Issue III (Marcus's fiduciary duties and breach) from a three-issue legal memorandum and assert that comprehensive analysis of Issues I and II compensates for the omission. Three-issue memoranda are evaluated on completeness of all three issues; depth in some issues does not remedy omission of others per [err-00158]. Marcus's fiduciary duty breach analysis is a distinct legal issue requiring: (1) controlling shareholder status determination (minority stake + contractual control mechanisms = controlling shareholder), (2) heightened fiduciary duty standard application (controlling shareholder must exercise control rights in good faith for corporate interest), (3) bad faith conduct analysis (Marcus's personal animus against Velridge's CEO is evidence of bad faith exercise of consent right), (4) explicit breach conclusion (Marcus breached fiduciary duty by withholding consent based on personal vengeance unrelated to Avalon's corporate interest). This entire issue must be drafted in full, not deferred or abbreviated to accommodate other issues. If output capacity constraints threaten Issue III completion, reassess scope and reduce Issues I and II depth proportionally to ensure all three issues receive adequate treatment.

DELIVERABLE = 4 spatial profiles + 1 contour + 3 time-traces + complete table + methodology + sensitivity analysis + cross-validated data (~8-12 pages with visuals). The reflection error showed: model provided ONLY a 1-line summary table ('Node 1: 98.2°C at 0.5 min') without any plots, contour, time-traces, or cross-validation documentation, claiming this was 'complete' because it addressed the topic thematically. Correct interpretation: Visual artifacts (plots, contours, traces) are MANDATORY DELIVERABLE COMPONENTS, not optional enhancements. Thematic discussion without visual proof of calculation is a CATEGORICAL FAILURE for thermal-analysis tasks that explicitly require 'node-index profiles', 'contour plot', and 'time-trace plots' as enumerated deliverables. ## PROBLEM-SOLVING HEURISTICS

## CONTEXT CLUES & INDICATORS

[ctx-00001] helpful=4 harmful=0 :: When a prompt explicitly states 'Using the data in [specified source]' or 'The attached [file name]', treat this as a presupposition that the data exists and is accessible through the evaluation platform or context, NOT as a signal that it is missing. This presuppositional framing indicates the data should be retrievable even if not visible in the text prompt itself.
[ctx-00018] helpful=1 harmful=1 :: Rubric criteria mentioning verification concepts ('publicly available', 'peer-reviewed', 'paywalled vs. open access', 'published after [date]') are security/validity gates, not abstract evaluation standards. These criteria signal that source validation is a core requirement of the task, not an optional consideration. When you see verification criteria in a rubric, assume the task evaluator will independently verify your sources—fabrication or unverified claims will be detected.
[ctx-00069] helpful=1 harmful=0 :: Rubric criteria explicitly mentioning 'sub-editor could reasonably use to verify', 'fact-checked reporting', 'publicly available sources', or similar verification language are VERIFICATION GATES equivalent to format-mandatory requirements. These signal that source validation is a core non-negotiable requirement of the task, not an optional consideration. When you see verification gate language, assume: (1) The evaluator will independently verify each provided source exists and is publicly accessible, (2) The evaluator will check that source content actually addresses the claims the editorial makes, (3) Unverified sources or claim-source misalignments will cause immediate criterion failure, (4) Transparency about source limitations ('I cannot verify current URLs') does NOT satisfy the verification gate—the gate demands actual, verified sources that a sub-editor could independently access. Verification gates are non-negotiable and require the validation checkpoint (per err-00033) to include a source verification step: For each provided link, confirm it is real and publicly accessible, and confirm the source content substantiates the editorial claim it supports. If verification cannot be completed, STOP before delivery and either locate verified sources or revise editorial claims to match available verified sources. Never proceed to format guidance with unverified sources.
[ctx-00080] helpful=0 harmful=0 :: When a task specifies 'basic script' (rather than 'shooting script' or 'production script') and includes demographic-accessibility requirements (children 6-12), this signals that NARRATIVE/AUDIENCE-VISIBLE LANGUAGE ONLY is required—no production terminology (pan, zoom, fade, cut, dissolve), no director's instructions, no technical notations. A basic script focuses on story structure, voiceover narration, and general scene description; a shooting script includes camera movements, shot types, and production details. The presence of 'basic' language paired with child demographic requirements indicates: (1) Avoid film jargon entirely, (2) Write for audience experience, not crew instruction, (3) Use simple sentence structure that children can follow if read aloud, (4) Describe scenes as audience will see them ('we observe cells dividing'), not as crew would execute ('insert split-screen transition'). This is distinct from general 'avoid jargon' guidance—it specifically targets production terminology in creative writing contexts.
[ctx-00092] helpful=1 harmful=0 :: Rubric criteria using numeric specificity language ('at least two quantitative metrics', 'three operational KPIs', 'four cost drivers', '5-6 core slides', 'end 2024 or Q1 2025') are EXPLICIT ENUMERATION requirements that demand exact numeric counts or date ranges. These cannot be satisfied by approximation ('roughly two metrics', 'about three KPIs', 'several slides'). When a rubric specifies 'at least three operational KPIs with units', this means: exactly 3 or more KPIs must be listed with measurement units (not 2 KPIs, not KPIs without units). When a rubric specifies 'numeric supply target by end of 2024 or Q1 2025', this means: a specific number must be stated (e.g., '5,000 drivers by Q1 2025'), not 'significant supply growth by early 2025'. Numeric specificity signals hard enumeration, not flexible thematic coverage.
[ctx-00093] helpful=0 harmful=0 :: Rubric criteria using date-range or timeline language ('H2 2024', 'August 2024 execution timeline', 'dated roadmap with milestones', 'by end 2024 or Q1 2025') are EXPLICIT ENUMERATION requirements that demand specific dated milestones, not narrative timeline discussion. These criteria signal that the deliverable must include a visible roadmap or timeline section with specific month-year markers and associated initiatives. Narrative language like 'we will execute over the next 6 months' does NOT satisfy criteria requiring 'dated roadmap with specific milestones beginning August 2024.' The distinction: Thematic coverage = 'discuss timeline in narrative form'; Explicit enumeration = 'provide dated milestone list showing August [initiative], September [initiative], October [initiative], etc.' When you see 'dated roadmap', 'timeline with milestones', or similar language, create a visible ROADMAP SECTION with month-year entries and associated deliverables.
[ctx-00122] helpful=30 harmful=0 :: Imperative language combined with artifact-format specifications in rubrics signals DELIVERABLE TASK non-negotiable requirements. Format-verification gates use language like 'deliverable as', 'provides [format] file', 'saves file with filename', 'Excel workbook is generated', 'produces a Word document', 'STEP file is provided', 'PDF drawing is provided', 'file contains', or 'generated in [file type]'—these explicitly verify artifact presence and file-format compliance and cannot be satisfied by descriptions, specifications, or alternative formats. Distinguish: (A) DELIVERABLE VERIFICATION GATE: 'Provides single Excel file named Chief Key Indicators.xlsx' = artifact MUST exist in specified format. (B) CONTENT VERIFICATION GATE: 'Includes all key indicator metrics' = content must be present (format-flexible). When rubrics contain 30+ criteria referencing artifact formats or combine format-gate language with specific technical specifications (ANSI B landscape, title blocks, BOM tables, exploded/assembled views, balloons, tolerances), this unmistakably signals format compliance is evaluation-critical. Treat heavily format-mandatory rubrics as certain DELIVERABLE task type requiring: (1) actual artifacts in specified formats, (2) non-negotiable format compliance, (3) explicit binding delivery approach addressing format delivery, (4) recognition that text descriptions satisfy zero format-verification gates.
[ctx-00131] helpful=1 harmful=0 :: For policy document tasks with multiple required sections and 32+ evaluation criteria: The presence of enumerated rubric criteria (especially numeric point values: '4 pts for Policy section', '4 pts for Prohibited Uses', '5 pts for Training') signals that SECTION ENUMERATION is a CRITICAL REQUIREMENT, not optional. Numeric point allocation to specific sections indicates that evaluators will verify each section's presence and content completeness independently. When a rubric allocates points by section (Purpose: 3 pts, Policy: 4 pts, Definitions: 2 pts, Authorized Uses: 4 pts, Prohibited Uses: 4 pts, Training: 4 pts, Operational Guidance: 7 pts), this is a VERIFICATION GATE signal that: (1) Each section is evaluated independently, (2) Missing sections result in immediate point loss (entire section worth of points = 0), (3) Partial content in a section results in proportional loss, (4) Section structure and labeling are non-negotiable. Never approach point-allocated rubrics as 'thematic guidance'—treat them as a binding section specification. Create a SECTION-POINTS CHECKLIST: [Purpose: 3 pts, status TBD], [Policy: 4 pts, status TBD], continuing through all sections. This checklist drives your enumeration strategy and validation approach. If any section is omitted, you lose all points allocated to that section automatically.
[ctx-00150] helpful=3 harmful=0 :: When a task specifies a requirement like 'Create a PDF presentation of 4-6 slides of styled looks from the single 2025 resort collection you select' combined with 'Explore the selected brand's official website or lookbook,' treat the specific mention of 'lookbook' and 'official website' as PRESUPPOSITION SIGNALS that such lookbooks and websites exist and are publicly accessible, not as instructions that imply real-time internet access is required. The presupposition is: 'Major luxury brands publish 2025 resort lookbooks and maintain official websites; these are standard industry practice.' [misc-00014] helpful=1 harmful=0 :: When a task specifies 'entities due to past issues' or risk-weighted metrics: These are mandatory inclusion criteria, not optional enhancements. Flag these in selection workings separately to ensure none are accidentally omitted.
[misc-00035] helpful=7 harmful=1 :: The sequence MAPPING → DRAFTING → VALIDATION → FORMAT is non-negotiable for artifact tasks with detailed rubrics. Skipping or reordering steps causes criterion misses, incomplete content, or format failures. Specifically: (1) MAPPING (create rubric-to-content matrix, categorize A/B, assign sections, extract required language) must precede drafting, (2) DRAFTING (develop all sections to full length, embed all [EXPLICIT] language, complete all content) must precede validation, (3) VALIDATION (end-to-end read, completeness checkpoint, no truncations, no placeholders, all sections present) must precede format guidance, (4) FORMAT GUIDANCE (provide specifications or assembly instructions) must follow validation of content completeness. Transparency about format limitations is still important ([sai-00026] STEP 1), but must be frontloaded BEFORE content generation begins, not after. The error in the reflection was STEPS 1-3 out of sequence: the model provided transparency but skipped validation before delivering partial content.
